Reset viewport emulation (preset \"desktop\") when finished.\n\n## What the surface is trying to accomplish\nIt is the index of coaching notes and session recaps a real human coach (Joshua) writes for a student. Per PRODUCT.md the strategic claim is \"a real coach is in it\" — a named human has met this student and looked at their work. Notes originate outside the app (sessions are on video, transcribed). Principle 2 is \"Compute the next step; do not present a grid.\" Principle 1 is \"Truthfulness over performance\": every number/status traces to real stored data, and an honest empty state beats invented content. Principle 5: \"The coach is not simulated.\"\n\n## What to produce\nThink like a design director. Be direct and specific, name exact elements. Cover:\n\n1. **AI slop verdict.** Would you believe \"AI made this\" on sight? Check against these absolute bans: side-stripe borders (border-left/right >1px as colored accent), gradient text, decorative glassmorphism, the hero-metric template, identical card grids, modal-as-first-thought. Also the project's own DESIGN.md bans: box-shadow on a Workroom surface, a second typeface in Workroom chrome, a serif crossing in, hex literals in components, colour used for anything but meaning.\n2. **Nielsen's 10 heuristics, each scored 0-4**, with the key issue per heuristic. Be honest; most real interfaces total 20-32/40.\n3. **Cognitive load**: count visible options at each decision point; flag >4. Is there progressive disclosure?\n4. **Emotional journey**: what does this surface evoke? Peak-end rule. Is it consistent with \"a real coach is in it\"?\n5. **What's working** (2-3 specific items, with why).\n6. **Priority issues** (3-5), each as: What / Why it matters / concrete Fix. Tag P0-P3.\n7. **Minor observations.**\n8. **Provocative questions** that might unlock a better solution.\n\nPay particular attention to: what the main content column actually contains at desktop width versus what the narrow side column contains; what a student with zero notes would see (read the code path, do not modify data); whether the page tells the student anything about what these items are or what to do next; and the information hierarchy between the sidebar, the panel list, and the stage.\n\nReport findings as text. Cite file:line where you can."}

Minor, but it is the one number on the page that does not trace\nto stored data.\n\n## Patterns and systemic issues\n\n1. **Alpha-based muted text is the contrast bug.** `rgba(ink, 0.4)` cannot be reasoned\n   about against a ground, and it is why one token breaks four separate places on one\n   page. DESIGN.md\n   already carries a \"known gaps\" appendix; this belongs in it either way.\n3. **Route-level cost is invisible at the provider.** A shell-mounted provider means a\n   text page silently pays a drill page's bill, and the only record of the cost is a\n   comment that is now wrong by 4-6x.\n\n## What is working\n\n- **The focus indicator is genuinely good.** Verified with a real keyboard Tab: a solid\n  2px ink outline at 2px offset, no fade-in, high contrast. # `$impeccable audit` — Notes and recaps

**11/20 — Acceptable, significant work needed.** Measured on live production, signed in, light theme only.

| Dimension | Score | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| Accessibility | 2/4 | Every date on the page renders at **2.10:1** |
| Performance | 1/4 | **1.79 MB** of question explanations on a 3-row text page |
| Responsive | 3/4 | Column swap is correct; main column is empty at ≥1280 |
| Theming | 2/4 | Two undefined tokens; documented body font never renders |
| Anti-patterns | 3/4 | No AI slop — genuinely restrained |

### The three that matter

**Every date fails AA at 2.10:1.** `--classroom-meta-soft` is `rgba(67,64,60,0.4)` in light — an alpha, not a resolved colour. It breaks the row dates, the count hint, the dateline and note descriptions in one stroke. It matters concretely: two of your three live notes are "Conditional Logic: the Cheat Sheet" and "…(five-section cut)", and the date is the only thing separating them. The fix already exists — `--classroom-meta-text: #6d6a67` is defined one line away and unused at these sites.

**1.79 MB transferred / 7.18 MB decoded, 44% of the page, on three rows of text.** `/classroom/recaps` isn't in `BANK_FREE_PREFIXES`, so it loads the whole LR bank plus one explanation fetch per PrepTest. I measured **59 requests, 33 of them 404s**. The comment on that denylist documents "9-14 explanation fetches" — it's stale by 4-6x. The bank grew and nobody re-measured.

**A student with no notes sees the title and nothing else.** `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:215` is a ternary with `: null` and no else branch. The rail is the same — `classroom-panel-empty` is gated on flagged and journal only. Against PRODUCT.md principle 1, and it lands hardest on a brand-new client whose first recap isn't written yet. Your own copy for it already exists in `RecapsSection.tsx:20`.

### Two corrections to things I said mid-run

The dark theme is **fine** (8.27:1 / 19.16:1) — my first dark screenshot caught a mid-transition frame. And the active row is fine at 10.31:1; I misread a small screenshot. Neither is a finding. Nothing persisted from that test: it was a CSS class flip in my tab, reverted, never your code or your saved preference.

The detector's one "side-stripe border" hit is also a **false positive** — it's a `blockquote` rule, which is the legitimate use.

### What's working

The focus ring is genuinely good — verified with a real keyboard Tab, 2px ink outline, no fade-in; the `:where()` floor from 2026-09-05 is doing exactly its job. The 1280px column swap is correct at all three widths, and I checked specifically for the double-read the `StageIndexList` comment claims to prevent — there isn't one. Unread state survives the broken accent token because it never relied on colour.

Full detail in [NOTES-RECAPS-AUDIT.md](projects/lsatu/NOTES-RECAPS-AUDIT.md), including four P2s (overlay isn't a dialog, redundant "Open the list", undefined tokens, Lexend never rendering) and three P3s.

The critique's second assessment is still running — I'll fold it in with the scored heuristics when it lands.

# Assessment A — LLM Design Review: "Notes and recaps" (`/classroom/recaps`)

**Method:** read `PRODUCT.md`, `DESIGN.md`, `src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md`, and the five named source files; inspected the live production page in my own tab (tab-2) in the shipped light theme at 1440×900 and 375×812, with measured computed styles and contrast ratios.

**Caveat on the data I saw:** I was signed in as Joshua (`is_admin`, role label "Teacher"), so the rail carried an extra "Teaching" group a student never sees, and the three notes present are his own test content — one is literally titled *"Verification: unread notes"* with the body *"Safe to delete."* That is not a design finding, but it does mean I could not observe a real student's note mix. Everything below about the zero-note path comes from reading the code, not from mutating data.

---

## 1. AI slop verdict

**No. This does not read as AI-made, and it is not close.** It reads as hand-built and opinionated — a warm paper room with hairline separation and nothing else. Against the absolute bans:

| Ban | Status |
|---|---|
| Side-stripe borders (>1px coloured leading edge) | **Clean** on this surface. The only >1px left borders in `classroom.css` are a blockquote rule (`classroom.css:2343`) and journal callouts (`:2704`, `:2749`), none of which render here. |
| Gradient text | **Clean.** No `background-clip: text` anywhere. |
| Decorative glassmorphism | **Clean** here. (`--observatory-glass*` exists at `classroom.css:876-880` but is scoped to `.classroom-root-drill`.) |
| Hero-metric template | **Clean.** There is no big number. The one metric — "All 3 read." — is a 15px sentence. |
| Identical card grids | **Clean.** Rows, not cards. No grid. |
| Modal-as-first-thought | **Mostly clean** — the drawer is a responsive fallback, not the primary. But see P1-4: below 1280px it fires *alongside* the inline list, which is modal-as-second-thought. |

Against the project's own DESIGN.md bans, three real hits:

- **A second typeface in Workroom chrome — confirmed.** `classroom.css:39` sets `.classroom-root { font-family: "Outfit", … }`, and `<body>` is Lexend. Measured: `h1.classroom-title` renders `Outfit` 700/36px; `body` renders `Lexend`. DESIGN.md's One Face Rule allows Outfit only as the *question lock* (LSAT stimulus and answer choices). This is the whole classroom shell, including the rail. DESIGN.md's sanctioned scoped-world list (`DESIGN.md:174`) names `.mc-bootcamp`, `.ff-bootcamp`, `.rc-bootcamp`, `.topic-abstract`, `.cs-punk` and the eight Activity worlds — `.classroom-root` is not on it, and DESIGN.md:156 says "Rooms may be worlds; corridors never are." The classroom rail *is* the corridor. Either DESIGN.md is stale here or the classroom is out of bounds; the record as written says out of bounds.
- **Colour used for something other than meaning — confirmed.** `--classroom-link: #ff8906` (`classroom.css:14`) fills the Messages unread badge; the panel-wash gradients (`classroom.css:130-146`) paint a decorative warm sheen on the rail and list card. Neither says correct, incorrect, warn, or danger.
- **A token that resolves to nothing — confirmed, and it is the exact failure DESIGN.md's Named Ground Rule was written for.** `.classroom-unread-dot` and `.classroom-unread-count` consume `var(--classroom-accent, …)` and `var(--classroom-surface, #fff)` at `classroom.css:4514`, `:4528-4529`. Neither variable is defined anywhere in `src/`. The unread dot therefore falls back to `currentColor` — the same ink as the note title next to it — so the "unread" marker renders as a bullet point. Meanwhile the *Messages* unread badge is orange. Two unread counters in one sidebar, two different colours, one of them accidental.

Clean on the rest: **zero box-shadows.** I enumerated every element under `.classroom-root` in the live page and got `shadowCount: 0`. The Flat Rule holds on this surface even though `ui/card.tsx` still ships `shadow-sm` app-wide (DESIGN.md gap #3). Motion is correct too — the stage-enter animation is inside `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` (`classroom.css:786`) and animates only `opacity`/`transform`.

**The real failure mode here is the opposite of slop.** Slop is over-decorated sameness. This is under-designed emptiness.

---

## 2. Nielsen's 10 heuristics — 21/40

**1. Visibility of system status — 2/4.** Read/unread is modelled honestly and sorted unread-first. But the only status sentence on the page, "All 3 read.", renders at **2.10:1 contrast** and is `display: none` below 1280px (`classroom.css:4484`), so on mobile the student gets no count at all. Nothing shows how many notes exist, when the last one arrived relative to their last session, or that more are coming.

**2. Match with the real world — 2/4.** "Notes and recaps" silently merges four DB types — `note`, `session_recap`, `announcement`, `smartboard_recap` (`classroomData.ts:12-17`) — and **no row shows which one it is.** The page title promises two categories and delivers an undifferentiated list. When you open one, the kind label is the raw enum with underscores swapped (`ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:168`: `item.type.replace(/_/g, " ")`), rendering lowercase "note" / "smartboard recap". "Smartboard recap" is internal vocabulary.

**3. User control and freedom — 2/4.** The section overlay (`ClassroomLayout.tsx:1104-1119`) and the mobile menu (`:1024-1053`) have **no `role="dialog"`, no `aria-modal`, no focus trap, no Escape handler, and no focus return.** The only `Escape` listener in the file is at `:160`, for the profile menu. A keyboard user can tab straight out of an open drawer into the page underneath it. No breadcrumb or back control in the note view; back is browser-only.

**4. Consistency and standards — 2/4.** The Flagged panel and the Wrong answers panel each get a search field (`ClassroomLayout.tsx:635`, `:779`) and each get an empty-state sentence (`:722`, `:822`). Notes gets neither. Same shell, same panel component, three different levels of finish. The `.classroom-empty-copy` class — named for empty states — is also used to render a note's real `description` (`ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:171`), so authored content inherits empty-state styling.

**5. Error prevention — 3/4.** Little to get wrong. Error and loading states exist and are real (`DetailError`, `DetailLoading`). Deducting only because the "This item is no longer available" fallback (`ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:144-161`) fires for *any* id miss including a still-loading cache, and offers only "Back to your room".

**6. Recognition over recall — 2/4.** A row is a title and a date, nothing else. To know whether "Conditional Logic: the Cheat Sheet" is a recap of your 23 Aug session or a standalone handout, you must open it. Two of the three rows I saw differ only by a parenthetical. No excerpt, no type, no length, no session link.

**7. Flexibility and efficiency — 1/4.** No search, no filter, no type toggle, no month grouping, no keyboard shortcut. Fine at three notes; unusable at forty session recaps a year in, which is exactly the trajectory of a 25-sessions-a-week practice. The sibling panels already solved this and the solution was not carried over.

**8. Aesthetic and minimalist design — 2/4.** The aesthetic is good — genuinely calm, warm, un-generic. The *proportion* is broken. At 1440px: sidebar 336px + section list 336px = **672px of chrome (47% of the viewport) before content**, and the 784×900 stage holds **89 pixels of content** (measured: dateline 22px, h1 41px, hint 25px). This is not minimalism; it is a layout that forgot to fill its largest region.

**9. Error recovery — 3/4.** `DetailError` gives a plain message and a working "Try again" that calls `query.refetch()`. Good. Deducting for the 27 uncaught 404s in the console on load and for `classroom-panel-search:focus { outline: none }` (`classroom.css:2503-2505`), which removes the focus ring and replaces it with a border-colour change — the exact thing DESIGN.md:290 says is too quiet to be the only indicator.

**10. Help and documentation — 2/4.** **The page says nothing about what these items are.** There is no sentence explaining that a coach writes them, that recaps follow sessions, or what to do with one. The only prose on the entire index is a count. Not zero, because the note bodies themselves teach well.

---

## 3. Cognitive load

**Decision point 1 — landing at desktop.** Visible options: 14 sidebar nav buttons for a student (17 for an admin), plus brand, profile trigger, Log Out, the panel heading, and 3 note rows. **~20 competing targets. Flag: >4 by 5×.** Zero of them are in the stage, which is where the eye goes first because it is the biggest, brightest, emptiest region on screen.

**Decision point 2 — which note.** 3 today, unbounded and unfiltered tomorrow. Rows are visually identical; the only discriminators are a title and a date at 2.10:1 contrast.

**Decision point 3 — inside a note.** **Zero options.** The note ends and the page stops. No Ask, no next, no related work, no mark-done, no back.

**Progressive disclosure:** structurally present (rail → list → item is a sound three-pane), but **inverted at desktop**. The 784px pane discloses 89px; the 336px pane carries the entire payload. The disclosure gradient runs the wrong way.

---

## 4. Emotional journey

**Arrival:** calm and slightly warm. The cream rail and the paper wash read as a study, not a SaaS dashboard. This is a real asset and it is rare.

**Two seconds in:** the eye lands on 700px of white with a 36px heading and one sentence too faint to read, and the feeling flips to *"did this fail to load?"* Blank space in the largest, brightest region does not read as restraint. It reads as absence.

**Opening a note:** the peak, and it is genuinely good. Real prose, a real argument, authored typography carried by the note's own `<style>` (`classroomData.ts:34-68`). This is the moment the coach exists.

**The end:** the note stops mid-air. No signature, no "ask me about this", no next thing. **Peak-end lands badly:** a strong peak followed by a dead stop, wrapped by an index that evokes emptiness.

**Against "a real coach is in it":** the index is the weakest link in the whole claim. Three rows of title-plus-date are indistinguishable from an RSS feed. Nothing signals a human wrote them, met this student, or looked at their work. `CONTEXT.md` deliberately says "the coach is never named in the furniture" and that is a defensible rule — but a list *of his writing* is not furniture, and the rule as applied has stripped the surface of the one thing PRODUCT.md says a competitor cannot copy.

---

## 5. What's working

1. **The read/unread model is Principle 1 done properly, including the hard part.** `classroomData.ts:81-89` documents that read state deliberately does *not* live in `status`, because the DB's `classroom_items_status_check` rejects the `'started'` value the TypeScript type declares. Rather than write a value the database would refuse, it stamps `metadata_json.read_at`. The marker is also never colour-only: `StageIndexList.tsx:52-57` pairs the dot with an `sr-only " (unread)"`, and `classroom.css:4501-4506` records the reasoning. This is the standard the rest of the surface should be held to.

2. **The panel and the page cannot disagree about ordering.** Both call the same `groupNotes` (`classroomData.ts:106-114`), and `ClassroomLayout.tsx:583-585` says why in one sentence. A whole class of "the list says one thing and the page says another" bug is designed out rather than tested for.

3. **The room is genuinely flat.** Measured 0 shadowed elements under `.classroom-root` on the live page. Depth comes from a 1px `--classroom-line` and one tonal step, exactly as DESIGN.md's Flat Rule asks — and this surface honours it while the shared `ui/card.tsx` still does not.

---

## 6. Priority issues

### P0-1 — The main content column is empty at desktop width
**What.** At ≥1280px, `.classroom-stage-index` is `display: none` (`classroom.css:4473-4477`), so `ClassroomRecapsView` renders only a dateline, an `<h1>`, and a one-line count into a 784×900 stage. Measured live at 1440×900: **89px of content in a 900px column.** The list the page is *for* lives entirely in the 336px side panel. The `StageIndexList` doc comment (`StageIndexList.tsx:12-25`) shows this was a deliberate fix for *narrow* windows — the wide case was never revisited.

**Why it matters.** The largest and brightest region of the screen is the one carrying the least information, so the page reads as broken on first sight. It also violates Principle 2 directly: a student arriving here is shown a menu in the periphery and told nothing in the centre.

**Fix.** Stop treating the stage as a placeholder that the panel happens to duplicate. Give the index route a stage worth landing on: the newest unread note **opened inline** (or its first ~150 words with a "read it" control), a one-line statement of what this surface is, and the "Ask" affordance `CONTEXT.md` already defines. If the intent is genuinely master-detail, then redirect `/classroom/recaps` to the newest note the way a mail client does, and let the panel be the index. Either is defensible; rendering an empty column is not. **P0.**

### P0-2 — Every piece of secondary text on the surface fails WCAG AA, measured in the shipped theme
**What.** `--classroom-meta-soft: rgba(67, 64, 60, 0.4)` (`classroom.css:25`) is used as a *text* colour. Measured in the browser on the live white ground:

| Element | Colour | Size | Contrast | AA |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dateline "September 8, 2026 \| Classroom" | rgba(67,64,60,.4) | 14px | **2.10:1** | fail |
| "All 3 read." (the page's only sentence) | rgba(67,64,60,.4) | 15px | **2.10:1** | fail |
| Every row date, "Aug 24, 2026" | rgba(67,64,60,.4) | 12px | **2.10:1** | fail |
| Rail group labels (CLASSROOM/STUDY/REVIEW) | rgb(166,162,158) | 12px | **2.54:1** | fail |
| Role label "Student"/"Teacher" | rgb(166,162,158) | 12px | **2.54:1** | fail |
| Messages unread badge, white on `#ff8906` | — | 11px | **2.38:1** | fail |
| `.classroom-panel-empty` (flagged/journal empties) | rgba(67,64,60,.4) | 13px | **2.10:1** | fail |

**Why it matters.** PRODUCT.md:99 makes AA a binding defect gate, not an aspiration. And `classroom.css:15-22` **already documents this exact problem and already ships the fix**: `--classroom-meta-text: #6d6a67` (5.38:1) and `--classroom-link-text: #a25704` (5.39:1), with the comment "Use these two instead whenever the colour carries words or notation." The badge carries a *number*. The tokens exist and are used 2 times against 15 uses of `--classroom-meta-soft` and 53 of `rgb(var(--classroom-meta))`.

**Fix.** Swap `--classroom-meta-soft` → `--classroom-meta-text` at every site where the value lands on text (`.classroom-dateline` `:2063`, `.classroom-empty-copy` `:2480`, `.classroom-panel-item time` `:2707`, `.classroom-panel-empty` `:2509`, `.classroom-nav-group > b`, `.classroom-profile-copy small`), and `--classroom-link` → `--classroom-link-text` on `.classroom-nav-badge` `:2299`. Keep `-soft` for rules and fills only. This is a token swap, not a redesign, and the file already argues for it. **P0.**

### P1-3 — A student with zero notes sees a completely blank page
**What.** Read the path. In `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:215`, everything below the heading is gated on `total > 0`, so at zero notes the stage renders *only* the dateline and the `<h1>` — no hint, no list, no copy. In parallel, `ClassroomLayout.tsx:717-727` renders `.classroom-panel-empty` **only when `section === "flagged"`**; the `notes` and `work` branches have no empty state at all. So a new student who clicks "Notes and recaps" gets a panel with a heading and nothing under it, beside a stage with a heading and nothing under it. Roughly 90% of the viewport is blank white.

**Why it matters.** This is a direct hit on Principle 1: *"an honest empty state beats invented content. If nothing has been assigned, the product says so and stops."* It does not say so. It stops without saying. And it is the surface a brand-new 1:1 student is most likely to click first, looking for evidence that a coach is on the other side.

**Fix.** The correct copy is already written elsewhere in the repo — `RecapsSection.tsx:20`: *"Recaps from your sessions with Joshua will show up here."* Render an empty state in **both** columns: the panel gets a `.classroom-panel-empty` line for `notes` and `work` (matching flagged and journal), and the stage gets a short paragraph saying what notes and recaps are, that Joshua writes them after sessions, and — if the student has a session booked — when the first one is expected. Nothing invented; all of it traces to real data. **P1.**

### P1-4 — Below 1280px the same list is offered twice, once inline and once in a drawer
**What.** Two mechanisms fire at the *same* breakpoint. `.classroom-stage-index` becomes `display: grid` at `max-width: 1279px` (`classroom.css:4479-4482`), and `.classroom-section-trigger` becomes `display: flex` at `max-width: 1279px` (`classroom.css:3044-3052`). Verified live at 375px: the stage shows the three notes inline *and*, above the `<h1>`, a full-width 48px button labelled "Open the list" whose drawer contains the same three notes. The trigger is also the **first** thing in the reading column, above the page title.

**Why it matters.** The student is offered a control to reach content already on screen. It is the only prominent button on the page, so it attracts the tap that should have gone to a note. It also puts a chrome control ahead of the page's own `<h1>` in both visual and DOM order.

**Fix.** The trigger exists for routes with no inline index (a note detail, an assignment). Suppress it on routes that render a `StageIndexList` — pass a flag through `ClassroomLayoutContext` from the index views, or scope the trigger's `display: flex` to `:not(:has(~ * .classroom-stage-index))`. Whichever, the invariant is: **never show both.** **P1.**

### P2-5 — A note is a dead end, and the row tells you nothing before you open it
**What.** `ClassroomItemView` (`ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:163-185`) renders heading, description, body, and stops. No author line, no "Ask", no link to the session it recaps, no next note, no back. And on the way in, a row (`StageIndexList.tsx:51-59`, `ClassroomLayout.tsx:665-682`) shows only title + date — no type, so a `session_recap`, an `announcement` and a `smartboard_recap` are indistinguishable on a page titled "Notes **and** recaps".

**Why it matters.** Principle 2 says compute the next step. The end of a note is the highest-intent moment on the surface — the student has just read something their coach wrote about them — and the product does nothing with it. `CONTEXT.md` already defines the affordance ("**Ask** — sending a question to the coach from the surface the student is looking at, with their own comment attached. Always labelled 'Ask'"), and this surface does not use it.

**Fix.** (a) Add the type as a quiet meta line on each row, using student-facing words, not the DB enum — "Session recap · 23 Aug" vs "Note · 24 Aug". Map `smartboard_recap` to something a student would say. (b) Put an "Ask" control at the foot of a note, prefilled with the note's title, routing to the existing 1:1 inbox thread. (c) Add "Next note" / "Back to notes and recaps" so the reading column is navigable on its own at every width. **P2.**

---

## 7. Minor observations

- **Overlay semantics.** `.classroom-section-overlay` and `.classroom-mobile-menu` use a `<button class="classroom-overlay-backdrop">` as scrim with no `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, focus trap, Escape handler, or focus restore (`ClassroomLayout.tsx:1024-1053`, `:1104-1119`). Roll them onto the Radix Dialog already in the dependency tree.
- **34px rail targets.** `.classroom-nav` computes to **34px** tall (`classroom.css:244-261`: 14px × 1.45 + 7px padding × 2). PRODUCT.md:104 requires 44×44 "by being that size rather than by a side effect". The same class draws the **mobile drawer**, where it is a genuine touch violation. The mobile menu button itself is a correct 44×44 (`classroom.css:2417-2422`).
- **The dateline on an index prints today's date.** `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:206` passes `new Date().toISOString()`, so the page reads "September 8, 2026 | Classroom" above a list whose newest item is 24 Aug. It looks like a publication date and dates nothing. Same pattern in `ClassroomUploadsView:251` and three not-found states. Drop the dateline on index routes.
- **Three identical strings on one screen.** "Notes and recaps" appears as the active rail item, the panel `<h2>`, and the stage `<h1>` simultaneously. DOM heading order is h2 → h3 → h3 → h3 → h1.
- **`<h3>` inside `<button>`** on every row, in both list copies. Works for the accessible name, but a heading that is also a control is unusual; `aria-label` on the button with a plain `<span>` inside would be cleaner.
- **27 uncaught 404s** in the console on page load, plus `Found 4 questions with unknown types`.
- **Notes can restyle themselves.** `sanitizeClassroomHtml` (`classroomData.ts:34-68`) deliberately allows `<style>`, and it is well-reasoned and well-commented. But it means each note can carry arbitrary CSS into the reading column — the one I opened rendered its own small-caps eyebrow. There is no containment (no `@scope`, no shadow root), so a note's `h2 { }` styles the room, not just the note.
- **`.classroom-panel-search:focus { outline: none }`** (`classroom.css:2503-2505`) removes the ring in favour of a border-colour change on the Flagged and Journal panels. Same shell, and DESIGN.md:290 names this specifically.
- **Hex literals in the stylesheet outside the token block:** `#ffffff` at `:290` and `:302`, `#fff` at `:4529`.

---

## 8. Provocative questions

1. **Is this page a page, or is it a redirect that hasn't admitted it?** Mail clients don't render an "Inbox" landing screen — they open the newest message. If the panel is the index at every width where it exists, what is `/classroom/recaps` actually *for*, and would the surface be better if it simply opened the newest unread note?

2. **The rule is "the coach is never named in the furniture." Is a list of his writing furniture?** Applied literally, the rule has produced an index of the coach's work in which the coach is invisible — the exact opposite of "a real coach is in it." Where is the seam between chrome and his voice, and does this surface fall on the wrong side of it?

3. **Why do notes arrive without a next step when everything else in the product computes one?** A note is the highest-intent artefact the platform holds — a human wrote it about this student. It ends in silence. What would the surface look like if every note ended with the one thing Joshua would say next?

4. **What breaks at forty notes?** The Flagged and Journal panels both got search. Notes did not. With ~25 sessions a week, this list is measured in hundreds within a year. Does it need search, or does it need something the other two don't — a session timeline, where a recap sits beside the work it produced?

5. **If "Notes and recaps" merges four record types, is the page named after the student's mental model or after a database filter?** `NOTE_TYPES` is a `Set` of four enums. A student does not think "note vs. smartboard recap." What are the two or three things they *do* think, and does the page's name and grouping match them?

6. **What is the classroom, architecturally?** DESIGN.md says corridors are never worlds, and the classroom rail is the corridor — yet the classroom ships its own typeface, its own warm palette, and its own orange accent. Either DESIGN.md needs a sanctioned entry for `.classroom-root` with its own brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/` (there is none — only `drill-hub.md` and `src-features-activity-centre.md`), or the classroom needs to come back to the Workroom's Lexend and neutrals. It is currently neither, and the gap is invisible until someone measures it.
</output>

Assessment A landed and independently confirms the core findings. It also surfaced several I hadn't measured. Let me verify those before they go into a plan.

Every contrast number below was measured in a browser against the\nactually-rendered background.\n\n---\n\n## Design Health Score — 21/40\n\n| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Visibility of system status | 2 | The only status line, \"All 3 read.\", is 2.10:1 and is `display:none` below 1280px, so mobile gets no count at all |\n| 2 | Match system / real world | 2 | Four DB types merged with no row showing which; detail shows the raw enum \"note\" |\n| 3 | User control and freedom | 2 | Overlay has no `role=\"dialog\"`, no focus trap, no Escape, no focus return |\n| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | Flagged and Journal panels both get search and an empty state; Notes gets neither |\n| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | Solid. \"No longer available\" also fires on a still-loading cache |\n| 6 | Recognition over recall | 2 | A row is a title and a date; two live rows differ only by a parenthetical |\n| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 1 | No search, filter, type toggle or grouping. Breaks at ~40 notes |\n| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 2 | Aesthetic is genuinely good; proportion is broken — 89px of content in a 900px column |\n| 9 | Error recovery | 3 | `DetailError` retry works. Marked down for 33 console 404s |\n| 10 | Help and documentation | 2 | The page never says what these items are or who writes them |\n| **Total** | | **21/40** | **Needs work** |\n\nCombined with the audit's **11/20**, the picture is consistent: the surface is\nwell-engineered and tastefully restrained, and it is under-designed and under-finished.\n\n## Anti-patterns verdict — pass\n\n**This does not look AI-generated, and it is not close.** Both assessments reached that\nindependently.\n\n- The deterministic detector returned **zero findings** on `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx` and\n  `StageIndexList.tsx`.\n- Verified live: **zero box-shadowed elements** under `.classroom-root`. The Flat Rule\n  holds on this surface even though the shared `ui/card.tsx` still ships `shadow-sm`.\n- No gradient text, no glassmorphism, no hero metric, no card grid, no modal-first.\n- Motion is correct: the stage-enter animation sits inside\n  `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` and animates only `opacity`/`transform`.\n- **The detector's one \"side-stripe border\" hit is a false positive** — it is a\n  `blockquote` rule, the legitimate use. Leave it.\n\nThe failure mode here is the opposite of slop. Slop is over-decorated sameness. This is\nunder-designed emptiness.\n\n---\n\n## The ledger\n\nEverything both assessments found, consolidated. This is the list the plan closes.\n\n| # | Finding | Sev | Where |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | All secondary text fails AA — 2.10:1 **[both]** | P0 | `classroom.css:25` + 6 call sites |\n| 2 | Rail group labels and role label at 2.54:1 | P0 | `--classroom-meta` sites |\n| 3 | Unread badge, white on `#ff8906`, 2.38:1 | P0 | `classroom.css:2299` |\n| 4 | Main column is empty at ≥1280px **[both]** | P0 | `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:201` |\n| 5 | Zero-note state renders nothing, in both columns **[both]** | P1 | `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:215`, `ClassroomLayout.tsx:717` |\n| 6 | Overlay is not a dialog: no role, trap, Escape or focus return **[both]** | P1 | `ClassroomLayout.tsx:1104`, `:1024` |\n| 7 | 1.79 MB / 59 requests of unused explanation JSON | P1 | `QuestionBankContext.tsx:37` |\n| 8 | 33 console 404s from sidecar probing | P1 | `questionLoader.ts:958` |\n| 9 | Below 1280px the list is offered twice **[both]** | P1 | `classroom.css:4479` + `:3044` |\n| 10 | Rail rows are 34px, under the 44px floor | P1 | `classroom.css:244` |\n| 11 | `.classroom-panel-search:focus { outline: none }` | P1 | `classroom.css:2503` |\n| 12 | Row never shows its type; 4 types look identical | P2 | `StageIndexList.tsx:51` |\n| 13 | A note is a dead end — no Ask, no next, no back | P2 | `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:163` |\n| 14 | `--classroom-accent`, `--classroom-surface` undefined **[both]** | P2 | `classroom.css:4514`, `:4529` |\n| 15 | Lexend never renders; two families, neither documented **[both]** | P2 | `classroom.css:39` |\n| 16 | No search, filter or grouping — breaks at ~40 notes | P2 | `ClassroomRecapsView` |\n| 17 | Note `<style>` is unscoped and can restyle the room | P2 | `classroomData.ts:34` |\n| 18 | Reading measure ~90ch on the detail view | P3 | `.classroom-prose` |\n| 19 | Raw DB enum shown to students | P3 | `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:167` |\n| 20 | Index page stamped with today's date **[both]** | P3 | `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:204` |\n| 21 | \"Notes and recaps\" printed three times at ≥1280px | P3 | shell + view |\n| 22 | `.classroom-empty-copy` styles real authored content | P3 | `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:171` |\n\n---\n\n## Two decisions that change the work\n\nEverything else is mechanical. These two are yours, and the plan branches on them.\n\n### Decision A — what is `/classroom/recaps` for?\n\nThe page renders 89px of content into a 900px column because the list lives in the rail.\nTwo defensible answers:\n\n- **A1 — Make it a real landing.** The stage opens the newest unread note inline (or its\n  first ~150 words with a \"Read it\" control), above a short line saying what this surface\n  is. Follows Principle 2, \"compute the next step\". More work; better answer.\n- **A2 — Admit it is a redirect.** `/classroom/recaps` redirects to the newest note, the\n  way a mail client opens the newest message, and the rail is the index. Much less work;\n  honest; loses the chance to say anything.\n\n**Recommendation: A1.** Principle 2 is explicit, and this is the surface where the \"a real\ncoach is in it\" claim is weakest. A2 is a legitimate fallback if you want this closed fast.\n\n### Decision B — is the classroom a \"world\" or a corridor?\n\n`classroom.css:39` overrides the body face to Outfit, so **Lexend renders nowhere** in the\nclassroom despite being downloaded. Roboto Mono is the second family, where DESIGN.md\ndeclares IBM Plex Mono. DESIGN.md says rooms may be worlds but *\"corridors never are\"*,\nand the classroom rail is a corridor. It is currently neither.\n\n- **B1 — Sanction it.** The classroom is a world. Add `.classroom-root` to DESIGN.md's\n  scoped-world list, write `.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md`, and swap Roboto Mono for\n  the documented IBM Plex Mono. Code stays; the record catches up.\n- **B2 — Bring it home.** Delete the `font-family` on `classroom.css:39` and let Lexend\n  through. The record stays; the code catches up. Visually this changes every classroom\n  surface, so it needs a look before it ships.\n\n**Recommendation: B1.** The classroom's warm palette and Outfit are deliberate and good —\nboth assessments called the aesthetic a genuine asset. The defect is that it is\nundocumented, not that it exists. B1 also costs far less and risks nothing visual.\n\n---\n\n## The plan\n\nNine phases, ordered so the cheapest highest-impact work lands first and nothing depends\non an unmade decision. Each phase is one commit and one verification.\n\n**A note on shipping:** per `PRODUCT.md`, deployment is not a side effect of pushing to\n`main` — production at lsatprep.study needs an explicit publish step. Every phase below\nends at \"merged\", not \"live\". Publishing stays your call.\n\n### Phase 1 — Contrast (closes 1, 2, 3) · P0 · ~1 hour\n\nThe whole fix is a token swap, and `classroom.css:15-22` already argues for it in a\ncomment: *\"Use these two instead whenever the colour carries words or notation.\"* The\ntext-safe tokens exist and are barely used.\n\n| Token | Measured on white | Use for |\n|---|---|---|\n| `--classroom-meta-soft: rgba(67,64,60,.4)` | **2.10:1** | rules and fills only, never text |\n| `--classroom-meta-text: #6d6a67` | **5.37:1** ✓ | any text |\n| `--classroom-link: #ff8906` | **2.38:1** as text | fills only |\n| `--classroom-link-text: #a25704` | **5.39:1** ✓ | any text or number |\n\n1. Swap `--classroom-meta-soft` → `--classroom-meta-text` at the text call sites:\n   `.classroom-dateline` (`:2063`), `.classroom-empty-copy` (`:2480`),\n   `.classroom-panel-item time` (`:2707`), `.classroom-panel-empty` (`:2509`).\n2. Darken the `--classroom-meta` triplet, or swap to `--classroom-meta-text`, for\n   `.classroom-nav-group > b` and `.classroom-profile-copy small` (both 2.54:1).\n3. `.classroom-nav-badge` (`:2299`): the number needs a text-safe ground. Either darken\n   the fill to `--classroom-link-text` with white text, or keep the orange fill and use\n   `--classroom-ink` for the numeral.\n4. Grep the remaining 15 `-soft` and 53 `rgb(var(--classroom-meta))` uses and split them\n   text vs. fill. Most are outside this surface but the token is shared, so this is where\n   the systemic fix happens.\n\n**Verify:** re-run the in-browser contrast probe. Every text node on\n`/classroom/recaps` and `/classroom/item/:id` at ≥4.5:1, normal text. No exceptions —\nPRODUCT.md makes AA a defect gate.\n\n### Phase 2 — Empty states (closes 5) · P1 · ~1 hour\n\n1. `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:215` — add the else branch. Copy already exists in the repo:\n   `RecapsSection.tsx:20`, *\"Recaps from your sessions with Joshua will show up here.\"*\n2. `ClassroomLayout.tsx:717` — un-gate `.classroom-panel-empty` from `section === \"flagged\"`\n   so `notes` and `work` get one too, matching Flagged and Journal.\n3. Keep it honest. Say what these are and who writes them. Do not invent a count, a\n   schedule, or a promise that does not trace to stored data.\n\n**Verify:** point the query at an empty array in a test render and confirm both columns\nsay something. Do not empty live data to test this.\n\n### Phase 3 — Overlay accessibility (closes 6, 11) · P1 · ~2 hours\n\nFixes the shared classroom overlay, so it lands on every section at once.\n\n1. Move `.classroom-section-overlay` and `.classroom-mobile-menu` onto the Radix Dialog\n   already in the dependency tree. That supplies `role=\"dialog\"`, `aria-modal`, the focus\n   trap, Escape, and focus restore in one move rather than four hand-rolled ones.\n2. Give each an accessible name.\n3. `classroom.css:2503` — delete `outline: none` on `.classroom-panel-search:focus`, or\n   ship a real ring in the same rule. DESIGN.md names this exact anti-pattern: a border\n   shift is too quiet to be the only indicator.\n\n**Verify:** keyboard only. Open the drawer, confirm focus moves in, Tab stays inside,\nEscape closes it, focus returns to the trigger. Confirm the search field shows a ring.\n\n### Phase 4 — Performance (closes 7, 8) · P1 · ~2 hours\n\n1. Grep the `/classroom/recaps` subtree for `questionLoader` per the rule written into\n   `QuestionBankContext.tsx`. The classroom shell is shared with `/flagged` and\n   `/journal`, which *do* read the bank, so the denylist entry must be the full\n   `/classroom/recaps`, never `/classroom`.\n2. Add it to `BANK_FREE_PREFIXES`.\n3. **Update the stale comment.** It documents \"9-14 explanation fetches\"; the measured\n   number is **59**. That comment is how the next person will reason about this.\n4. Separately, make `mergeExplanationSidecars` read a manifest of which PrepTests have\n   sidecars instead of probing all of them. That kills 33 404s everywhere, not just here.\n\n**Verify:** clean load of `/classroom/recaps`, then confirm zero `/data/explanations/`\nrequests and page transfer down from 4.08 MB. Then load `/flagged` and `/journal` and\nconfirm questions still resolve — that is the regression this change risks.\n\n### Phase 5 — The duplicate list (closes 9, 21) · P1 · ~1 hour\n\nTwo rules fire at the same breakpoint: `.classroom-stage-index` becomes `grid` at\n≤1279px and `.classroom-section-trigger` becomes `flex` at ≤1279px. The trigger is also\nthe first thing in the reading column, above the `<h1>`.\n\n1. Suppress the trigger on any route that renders its own `StageIndexList`. Cleanest is a\n   flag through `ClassroomLayoutContext` set by the index views.\n2. The invariant to hold: **never show both.**\n3. While here, drop one of the three simultaneous \"Notes and recaps\" strings at ≥1280px.\n\n**Verify:** at 375, 900, 1134, 1440. Exactly one list, and the trigger appears only where\nthere is no inline index (a note detail, an assignment).\n\n### Phase 6 — Touch targets (closes 10) · P1 · ~30 min\n\n`.classroom-nav` computes to **34px** (14px × 1.45 + 7px × 2). PRODUCT.md requires 44×44\n*\"by being that size rather than by a side effect\"*. The same class draws the mobile\ndrawer, where it is a real touch failure.\n\nRaise the padding so the row is 44px by construction. The mobile menu button is already a\ncorrect 44×44 and needs nothing.\n\n### Phase 7 — The stage (closes 4, 20, 22) · P0 · ~half a day · **depends on Decision A**\n\nUnder **A1**: the stage opens the newest unread note inline, or its opening ~150 words\nwith a \"Read it\" control, above one line saying what this surface is and who writes it.\nUnder **A2**: `/classroom/recaps` redirects to the newest note and the rail is the index.\n\nEither way:\n- Drop the dateline from index routes (`ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:204`). Today's date above\n  a list whose newest item is 24 Aug dates nothing, and it is the one number on the page\n  that traces to `Date.now()` rather than stored data.\n- Stop rendering a note's real `description` in `.classroom-empty-copy`\n  (`ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:171`). Authored content should not inherit empty-state styling.\n\n### Phase 8 — Typography and tokens (closes 14, 15) · P2 · ~1–2 hours · **depends on Decision B**\n\nUnder **B1** (recommended): add `.classroom-root` to DESIGN.md's scoped-world list, write\n`.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md` recording Outfit + the warm palette as deliberate, and\nswap Roboto Mono → IBM Plex Mono (already loaded, already the documented mono).\nUnder **B2**: delete `classroom.css:39` and review every classroom surface on Lexend.\n\nIndependently of A or B:\n- Define `--classroom-accent` and `--classroom-surface`, or delete the two `var()` calls\n  that reach for them. Right now the unread dot falls back to `currentColor` and renders\n  as the same ink as the title beside it, while the Messages badge is orange — two unread\n  markers, two colours, one of them accidental. DESIGN.md: *\"a token that resolves to\n  nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect.\"* That record already lists three names that\n  pointed at nothing for five months.\n- Remove the hex literals at `classroom.css:290`, `:302`, `:4529`.\n\n### Phase 9 — Content and scale (closes 12, 13, 16, 17, 18, 19) · P2/P3 · ~half a day\n\n1. **Show the type on each row.** \"Session recap · 23 Aug\" vs \"Note · 24 Aug\". Four DB\n   types currently render identically on a page whose title promises two categories.\n2. **Map the enums to student words.** `smartboard_recap` is internal vocabulary; the\n   detail view currently prints the raw column value.\n3. **End a note with something.** `CONTEXT.md` already defines the affordance — \"Ask\",\n   always labelled \"Ask\", carrying the student's comment from the surface they are on.\n   Route it into the existing 1:1 inbox thread, prefilled with the note title. Add\n   \"Next note\" and \"Back to notes and recaps\".\n4. **Constrain `.classroom-prose`** to 65–75ch. It currently runs ~90ch at 720px/16px,\n   against DESIGN.md's own Reading Measure Rule.\n5. **Scale.** At ~25 sessions a week this list is in the hundreds within a year, and it\n   has no search, filter or grouping while Flagged and Journal both do. Month grouping is\n   probably the better answer than search for a chronological archive — but this is worth\n   its own `$impeccable shape` rather than a guess.\n6. **Scope note `<style>`.** Allowing it is well-reasoned and documented, but there is no\n   containment, so a note's `h2 {}` styles the room. `@scope` or a shadow root.\n\n---\n\n## Sequencing\n\n```\nPhase 1  Contrast          P0  ──┐\nPhase 2  Empty states      P1    │  independent, ship in any order\nPhase 3  Overlay a11y      P1    │\nPhase 4  Performance       P1    │\nPhase 5  Duplicate list    P1    │\nPhase 6  Touch targets     P1  ──┘\nPhase 7  The stage         P0  ←── needs Decision A\nPhase 8  Typography        P2  ←── needs Decision B\nPhase 9  Content + scale   P2/P3 ←── after Phase 7\n```\n\nPhases 1–6 close 11 of the 22 findings including all three P0 contrast items, need no\ndecisions, and are mostly token swaps and prop threading. That is the first day's work.\n\n## Definition of done\n\n- Every text node on both routes measures ≥4.5:1 **in a browser**, not in the token file.\n- A zero-note student sees an honest sentence in both columns.\n- Keyboard only: drawer traps focus, Escape closes, focus returns, search shows a ring.\n- Clean load of `/classroom/recaps` makes **zero** `/data/explanations/` requests, and\n  `/flagged` and `/journal` still resolve questions.\n- Exactly one list at every width from 375 to 1440.\n- No rail target under 44px.\n- Re-run `$impeccable audit` (target: 11/20 → 17+/20) and `$impeccable critique`\n  (target: 21/40 → 30+/40).\n"}

# `$impeccable critique` — 21/40, and the plan

Both assessments ran in isolation and never saw each other. They converged on seven findings independently — that agreement is the strongest signal in the report.

**Anti-patterns: pass.** Both concluded this does not look AI-generated. The detector returned zero findings on the two view files, and I measured **zero box-shadowed elements** under `.classroom-root`. The one "side-stripe" hit is a `blockquote` — false positive. As the design review put it: the failure mode here is the opposite of slop. Slop is over-decorated sameness; this is under-designed emptiness.

**Where it scores worst:** Flexibility 1/4 (no search, filter or grouping — Flagged and Journal both got one, Notes didn't, and at 25 sessions/week this list is in the hundreds within a year). Then five 2s.

### What the second assessment caught that I missed

I'd only measured the stage. It measured the whole shell and found more AA failures, which I then verified myself:

- Rail group labels (CLASSROOM/STUDY/REVIEW) — **2.54:1**
- Role label "Student"/"Teacher" — **2.54:1**
- Messages unread badge, white on `#ff8906` — **2.38:1**
- Rail rows are **34px** tall against PRODUCT.md's 44px floor — and the same class draws the mobile drawer, so that's a real touch failure
- `.classroom-panel-search:focus { outline: none }` — the exact anti-pattern DESIGN.md names by hand

It also found the fix I'd under-estimated: `--classroom-meta-text: #6d6a67` measures **5.37:1**, and `classroom.css:15-22` already contains a comment arguing for exactly this swap. The tokens exist and are used twice against 15 uses of the broken one. **Phase 1 is a token swap, not a redesign.**

### The plan

22 findings, 9 phases. Phases 1–6 close 11 of them including all three P0 contrast items, need no decisions from you, and are mostly token swaps and prop threading — that's roughly the first day.

Two things I did *not* decide for you, because they change the work materially:

**A — what is `/classroom/recaps` for?** It renders 89px of content into a 900px column. Either make it a real landing (newest unread note inline + one line of orientation), or admit it's a redirect and open the newest note like a mail client. I recommend the first; Principle 2 is explicit and this is where "a real coach is in it" is weakest.

**B — is the classroom a world or a corridor?** `classroom.css:39` overrides the body face, so Lexend renders nowhere in the classroom despite being downloaded, and Roboto Mono stands in for the documented IBM Plex Mono. Either sanction it in DESIGN.md and swap the mono, or delete line 39 and come back to Lexend. I recommend sanctioning it — both assessments called the warm Outfit aesthetic a genuine asset. The defect is that it's undocumented, not that it exists.

Full detail in [NOTES-RECAPS-PLAN.md](projects/lsatu/NOTES-RECAPS-PLAN.md).

One thing worth flagging before any of this ships: per PRODUCT.md, pushing to `main` doesn't deploy — lsatprep.study needs the explicit publish step. Every phase in the plan ends at "merged", not "live".

{"action": "javascript_exec", "text": "\nfunction parse(c){const m=c.match(/[\\d.]+/g).map(Number);return{r:m[0],g:m[1],b:m[2],a:m[3]===undefined?1:m[3]};}\nfunction over(f,b){return{r:f.a*f.r+(1-f.a)*b.r,g:f.a*f.g+(1-f.a)*b.g,b:f.a*f.b+(1-f.a)*b.b,a:1};}\nfunction lum(c){const f=v=>{v/=255;return v<=0.03928?v/12.92:Math.pow((v+0.055)/1.055,2.4)};return 0.2126*f(c.r)+0.7152*f(c.g)+0.0722*f(c.b);}\nfunction ratio(a,b){const l1=lum(a),l2=lum(b);const[h,l]=l1>l2?[l1,l2]:[l2,l1];return +((h+0.05)/(l+0.05)).toFixed(2);}\n// start at the element ITSELF so a self-painted fill counts\nfunction bgOf(el){let n=el;while(n&&n!==document.documentElement){const p=parse(getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor);if(p.a>0)return p;n=n.parentElement;}return{r:255,g:255,b:255,a:1};}\n// effective opacity from ancestors too\nfunction effOpacity(el){let o=1,n=el;while(n&&n!==document.documentElement){o*=+getComputedStyle(n).opacity;n=n.parentElement;}return o;}\n\nconst b = document.querySelector('.classroom-nav-group > b');\nconst badge = document.querySelector('.classroom-nav-badge');\nconst active = document.querySelector('.classroom-nav-active');\nfunction report(el,label){\n  if(!el) return {label,missing:true};\n  const cs=getComputedStyle(el);\n  const bg=bgOf(el);\n  let fg=parse(cs.color); const o=effOpacity(el);\n  return {label, color:cs.color, opacity:o.toFixed(2), ownBg:cs.backgroundColor,\n          groundUsed:`rgb(${Math.round(bg.r)},${Math.round(bg.g)},${Math.round(bg.b)})`,\n          ratio: ratio(over({...fg,a:fg.a*o},bg),bg)};\n}\nout={\n  groupLabel: report(b,'nav group label (B)'),\n  badge: report(badge,'unread badge'),\n  activeItem: report(active,'active nav item'),\n  probeApplied: !!document.getElementById('phase1-probe'),\n  metaTextToken: getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('.classroom-root')).getPropertyValue('--classroom-meta-text')\n};\nout\n"}

/* Unread count on a rail item. Sits where the `em` chevron sits on the section
   rows, so nav labels stay on one baseline whether an item is counted or not. */
.classroom-root .classroom-nav-badge {
  align-items: center;
  background: var(--classroom-badge-fill);
  border-radius: 999px;
  color: var(--classroom-badge-ink);
  display: inline-flex;
  flex: 0 0 auto;
  font-family: "Roboto Mono", ui-monospace, monospace;
  font-size: 0.6875rem;
  font-weight: 600;
  justify-content: center;
  line-height: 1;
  margin-left: auto;
  min-width: 18px;
  padding: 3px 5px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-nav em {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-text);
  font-family: "Roboto Mono", ui-monospace, monospace;
  font-size: 0.75rem;
  font-style: normal;
  margin-left: auto;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-nav-active em {
  color: var(--classroom-active-ink);
  opacity: 0.6;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-profile {
  align-items: stretch;
  border-top: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 8px;

## assistant

Phase 1 is implemented and verified. Let me show you where it stands.

## Phase 1 complete — contrast

**Changed** in `classroom.css`:
- **64 text sites** swapped from the fill tokens (`--classroom-meta-soft`, `rgb(var(--classroom-meta))`) to `--classroom-meta-text`. The 4 border/fill uses were left alone — that's the split the file's own comment already asked for.
- **New `--classroom-badge-fill` / `--classroom-badge-ink` pair.** I couldn't just reuse `--classroom-link-text`: it's `#a25704` in light but a bright `#ff8906` in dark, so reusing it would have traded a light failure for a dark one. Each theme now picks the pairing its own ground allows — 5.38:1 light, 8.06:1 dark.
- **Removed 5 `opacity` declarations** on muted text. This was the part neither assessment caught: the group labels were already going to be `#6d6a67`, but a `0.70` opacity multiplied on top and held them at 2.92:1. A token swap alone would not have fixed the sidebar.

**Verified** on the live app with those exact rules applied:

| Route | Nodes checked | Failures | Minimum |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/classroom/recaps` | 44 | **0** | **4.90:1** |
| `/classroom/item/:id` (chrome) | 39 | **0** | **4.90:1** |

CSS parses clean at 646 rules. In the screenshot the dates, the group labels and the badge are all legible where they were washed out before.

Two things you should know rather than take on trust:

**The verification was a faithful DOM check, not a build.** I applied the exact rule changes to the live page and measured every text node. I could not serve my own build — port 8080 is held by another chat's dev server, and I won't kill someone else's. Nothing I did persisted; it was a CSS injection in my browser tab only.

Along the way I caught myself twice: my first probe used `!important` too broadly and reported the active-nav chevron at 1.49:1. That was my measurement breaking the cascade, not the file — `.classroom-nav-active em` already carries the right colour and wins on specificity. Corrected before I drew any conclusion from it.

### A new finding, and it's a real one

On the note detail I split chrome from authored content:

- Chrome: 39 nodes, **0 failures**
- The note's own content: 564 nodes, **20 failures, worst 2.03:1**

The "Conditional Logic: the Cheat Sheet" note carries inline styles with text at 2.03:1 — "sufficient", "necessary", "left", "right". That's your teaching content, not `classroom.css`, so no token change reaches it. It's ledger item #17 with a price tag attached, and it's arguably P1 on its own since it's student-facing LSAT material below the AA gate.

I have not committed. Nine files including `classroom.css` already had your uncommitted changes in them, and I won't sweep those into a commit of mine.

## assistant

Two corrections you need before the next step, both mine.

## I was wrong about your note content

I told you the Cheat Sheet note had **20 elements at 2.03:1** and called it arguably P1. That was a bug in my measuring probe, not a problem in your content. I was treating any background with alpha > 0 as opaque, so a `rgba(0,0,0,0.04)` tint over white got scored as solid black.

Re-measured with the ancestor background stack composited properly: **one** failure in 564 authored text nodes — a 10px uppercase label ("the trigger") at **3.90:1** on a grey chip.

Your note also authors against the design tokens (`var(--classroom-meta-text)`, `var(--classroom-link-text, #a25704)`) rather than hard-coding hex. That means Phase 1's token fix reaches your note content on its own.

**So the floor you approved isn't justified.** One 10px label at 3.90:1 doesn't warrant a blanket override on `.classroom-prose`, and a floor would flatten the colour you're using pedagogically — those "sufficient"/"necessary"/"left"/"right" labels are deliberate. I haven't built it. I'd fix the one label in the source note instead.

## The same bug made the chrome look *better* than it is

Phase 1's before-state was worse than I reported: the sidebar group labels are **1.85:1**, not 2.54:1, once the 0.7 opacity is counted. Phase 1 takes them to 5.37:1.

I re-ran the Phase 1 verification under the corrected probe. It holds:

| Route | Nodes | Failures | Min |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/classroom/recaps` | 44 | **0** | **4.90:1** |
| production, before | 39 | 14 | 1.85:1 |

Both docs are corrected on disk.

## The port workaround doesn't work

I added a config on 8081 with `--strictPort` and the harness still refused with the same 8080 message. It's guarding at the project level — one registered dev server per repo — not checking my port. So "run on another port" isn't available to me from this session.

Real options: stop the other chat's server so I can start mine, or I keep implementing Phases 2–6 and we do one verification pass when a server frees up. Given CLAUDE.md calls unverified UI changes the top recurring failure, and Phases 2–6 are TSX where I can't simulate in the DOM the way I did for CSS, I'd rather have the server.

Phase 1 is done and verified; nothing is committed, since nine files including `classroom.css` already carried your uncommitted work.

## assistant

Server's up and Phase 1 verifies from the actual build.

## Dev server fixed

The problem wasn't my config. The harness reads the **vault-root** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\.claude\launch.json`, not the repo's — it has a single `lsatu-dev` entry pinned to 8080 with no `autoPort`. I'd been editing the wrong file. Added `autoPort: true` there (it's a plain Vite server, nothing needs 8080 specifically, and `vite.config.ts:33` already honours `PORT`). It took port **54409**; the other chat keeps 8080. No build errors.

I also reverted my earlier `vite-dev-8081` config in the repo — it had hardcoded `--port` flags, the exact anti-pattern the error message warned about.

## Phase 1 verified against the served build

| Assertion | Result |
|---|---|
| Text sites still on a fill token | **0** |
| Text sites now on `--classroom-meta-text` | 68 |
| `border-color` fill uses preserved | 4 |
| `--classroom-badge-fill/ink`, both themes | present, rule uses them |
| 3 opacity removals | gone |
| `.classroom-nav-active em` opacity | deliberately kept |

One more false alarm from my own tooling along the way: an unanchored regex counted `border-color:` as `color:` and reported 4 leftovers. Re-checked with a proper boundary — genuinely zero.

## What still isn't verified, and why

`/classroom/recaps` is behind auth, and localhost:54409 has no Supabase session. **I won't sign in as you** — that's a line I don't cross, even to finish a verification.

So Phase 1 currently rests on two halves: the build serves the right CSS (proven above), and the colour maths is correct on the real rendered page (proven earlier against production, 44 nodes, 0 failures, min 4.90:1). What's missing is seeing my build render that route.

That gap matters more for Phases 2–6 than it did for Phase 1 — those change TSX, where I can't substitute a DOM measurement the way I could for a stylesheet, and CLAUDE.md is explicit that unverified UI changes are the top recurring failure here.

The unblock is one action from you: sign in at **http://localhost:54409** in the Browser pane, and the session persists for the rest of the session's verification. Want to do that now, or should I implement Phases 2–6 and batch the verification once you've logged in?

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\components\\classroom\\shell\\useDialogOverlay.ts", "content": "import * as React from \"react\";\n\n/**\n * Modal semantics for the classroom's two hand-built overlays: the mobile menu\n * and the section list drawer.\n *\n * Both shipped as a backdrop <button> beside an <aside> carrying no attributes\n * at all. Measured before this hook existed: opening one left focus on the\n * element behind it, Escape did nothing, and Tab walked straight out of the\n * drawer into the page underneath, which is still rendered and still scrollable.\n *\n * Radix Dialog is in the tree and would supply all of this. It is deliberately\n * not used here: nine CSS rules key off `.classroom-section-overlay` and\n * `.classroom-mobile-menu`'s exact structure, and this file already hand-rolls\n * the same pattern for the profile menu. Matching the local idiom keeps the\n * change to behaviour rather than to markup and styling.\n *\n * Returns props to spread on the panel element. The caller keeps ownership of\n * open/close so the existing backdrop and close-button handlers still work.\n */\nexport function useDialogOverlay({\n  open,\n  onClose,\n  label,\n}: {\n  open: boolean;\n  onClose: () => void;\n  label: string;\n}) {\n  const panelRef = React.useRef<HTMLElement | null>(null);\n  // Captured at open, restored at close. Without this, dismissing the drawer\n  // drops focus to <body> and a keyboard user restarts from the top of the page.\n  const returnTo = React.useRef<HTMLElement | null>(null);\n\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    if (!open) return;\n    returnTo.current = document.activeElement as HTMLElement | null;\n\n    const focusables = () =>\n      Array.from(\n        panelRef.current?.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(\n          'a[href], button:not([disabled]), input:not([disabled]), select:not([disabled]), textarea:not([disabled]), [tabindex]:not([tabindex=\"-1\"])',\n        ) ?? [],\n      ).filter((el) => el.offsetParent !== null);\n\n    // Move focus in on the next frame: the panel animates in, and focusing a\n    // node that is still display:none silently does nothing.\n    const raf = requestAnimationFrame(() => {\n      const first = focusables()[0];\n      (first ?? panelRef.current)?.focus();\n    });\n\n    const onKeyDown = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {\n      if (event.key === \"Escape\") {\n        event.stopPropagation();\n        onClose();\n        return;\n      }\n      if (event.key !== \"Tab\") return;\n      const items = focusables();\n      if (items.length === 0) {\n        event.preventDefault();\n        return;\n      }\n      const first = items[0];\n      const last = items[items.length - 1];\n      const active = document.activeElement;\n      // Wrap at both ends, and pull focus back in if it has already escaped.\n      if (event.shiftKey && (active === first || !panelRef.current?.contains(active))) {\n        event.preventDefault();\n        last.focus();\n      } else if (!event.shiftKey && active === last) {\n        event.preventDefault();\n        first.focus();\n      }\n    };\n\n    document.addEventListener(\"keydown\", onKeyDown);\n    return () => {\n      cancelAnimationFrame(raf);\n      document.removeEventListener(\"keydown\", onKeyDown);\n      // Only steal focus back if it is still inside the panel being unmounted.\n      // Opening a note from the drawer moves focus deliberately; restoring here\n      // would yank it back to the trigger the student just used.\n      const active = document.activeElement;\n      if (!active || active === document.body || panelRef.current?.contains(active)) {\n        returnTo.current?.focus();\n      }\n    };\n  }, [open, onClose]);\n\n  return {\n    ref: panelRef as React.RefObject<any>,\n    role: \"dialog\" as const,\n    \"aria-modal\": true,\n    \"aria-label\": label,\n    tabIndex: -1,\n  };\n}\n"}

{"action": "javascript_exec", "tabId": "seed", "text": "\nconst results={};\nconst panel=()=>document.querySelector('.classroom-section-overlay aside');\nconst foc=()=>[...panel().querySelectorAll('a[href],button:not([disabled]),[tabindex]:not([tabindex=\"-1\"])')].filter(e=>e.offsetParent!==null);\n\n// --- Tab trap: from LAST element, Tab should wrap to FIRST\nlet items=foc();\nresults.focusableInPanel=items.length;\nitems[items.length-1].focus();\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Tab',bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,150));\nresults.tabFromLastWrapsToFirst = document.activeElement===foc()[0];\n\n// --- Shift+Tab from FIRST should wrap to LAST\nitems=foc(); items[0].focus();\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Tab',shiftKey:true,bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,150));\nitems=foc();\nresults.shiftTabFromFirstWrapsToLast = document.activeElement===items[items.length-1];\n\n// --- focus escaped outside? pull it back\ndocument.querySelector('.classroom-sidebar button')?.focus();\nresults.focusParkedOutside = !panel().contains(document.activeElement);\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Tab',shiftKey:true,bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,150));\nresults.pulledBackIn = panel()?panel().contains(document.activeElement):false;\n\n// --- Escape closes and restores focus to the trigger\nconst trig=document.querySelector('.classroom-section-trigger');\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Escape',bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,600));\nresults.escapeClosed = !document.querySelector('.classroom-section-overlay');\nresults.focusRestoredToTrigger = document.activeElement===trig;\nresults.activeAfterClose=(document.activeElement.className||document.activeElement.tagName).toString().slice(0,36);\nout=results;\nout\n"}

## assistant

Typecheck clean. Here's where Phases 1–4 stand, including one claim I have to walk back.

## Done and verified

**Phase 1 — contrast.** Verified on my build, real route, no probe: 44 text nodes, **0 failures, min 4.90:1**. Before was 14 failures at **1.85:1**.

**Phase 2 — empty states.** Forced the query cache to empty and watched both columns render "Notes and recaps from your sessions with Joshua will show up here." Rows 6→0, hint gone. Your data restored immediately (6 rows, hint back). The copy is lifted from `RecapsSection.tsx` so the two can't drift — but the *Assigned work* line ("Work Joshua assigns you will show up here") is mine, and PRODUCT.md says route student-facing copy to you. Please confirm or replace it.

**Phase 3 — overlay a11y.** New `useDialogOverlay` hook, verified on both overlays:

| | Section drawer | Mobile menu |
|---|---|---|
| `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal` / label | ✓ | ✓ |
| Focus moves in on open | ✓ | ✓ |
| Tab / Shift+Tab wrap | ✓ | — |
| Focus pulled back if it escapes | ✓ | — |
| Escape closes | ✓ | ✓ |
| Focus restored to trigger | ✓ | ✓ |

I deviated from the plan here: it said migrate to Radix Dialog, I hand-rolled a hook instead. Nine CSS rules key off the existing overlay markup and the file already hand-rolls this pattern for the profile menu, so this keeps the change to behaviour rather than markup on a shell another chat is also working in.

Also fixed `.classroom-panel-search:focus { outline: none }` — that was overriding the global focus floor.

Testing Phase 3 exposed a real bug in my own hook: I used `requestAnimationFrame` to move focus, which never fires while the pane isn't rendering. Switched to a timer, which also fixes background tabs.

## Phase 4 — implemented, and I could not verify it

I told you the explanation requests went **59 → 0** and that this proved the fix. **That was wrong.** I ran the control: with my denylist entry *removed*, dev still fires 0. The dev server never loads the question bank on any route, so the 0 was environmental, not my change.

I chased it — the bank is `public/data/_bank.generated.json`, generated by `prebuild`, which was missing locally. I generated it (5.54 MB, tracked, regenerated identically, no repo dirt). Still 0 on both routes. Something else gates the bank in dev.

So Phase 4 rests on reasoning, not measurement:
- Grep-verified: nothing this route mounts imports `questionLoader` (shell, view, `StageIndexList`, `classroomData`, `useClassroomItems`, `useAssignedTodoFeed`, `flaggedService`).
- The prefix is the full `/classroom/recaps`, so it cannot match `/flagged` or `/journal`.
- I corrected the stale "9-14 explanation fetches" comment to the measured 59.

Verifying it needs a production build, and `npm run build` fails on Windows — `NODE_OPTIONS=... vite build` is bash syntax that cmd rejects. That's a pre-existing repo issue, unrelated to my changes.

**One control finding worth keeping:** `/flagged` shows raw QIDs (`PT133-S3-Q26`) instead of question labels on *production too*. Pre-existing, not mine, and not on the recaps surface — but it's a real defect.

[]);\n  const total = unread.length + read.length;\n  // Unread first, because the reason to open this page is almost always\n  // something that just arrived. Falls back to the newest read note so the\n  // stage still says something once the student is caught up.\n  const featured = unread[0] ?? read[0] ?? null;"}

Flagged on 2026-09-09 on the\nNotes and recaps page, where a note read \"The same content in five doors instead of\neleven.\"\n\n**Why:** Joshua reads it as an obvious AI tell. {"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\.impeccable\\surfaces\\classroom.md", "content": "# Surface brief: the classroom (`.classroom-root`)\n\n**Scope.** Everything behind the `.classroom-root` wrapper: the rail, the section list,\nthe stage, and the routes under `/classroom`, `/flagged` and `/journal` that render\ninside that shell. Not the bootcamps or the Activity Centre worlds, which have their own\nwrappers.\n\n**Register.** Product. Design serves the work; the student is here to read what their\ncoach wrote and to find the next thing to do.\n\n**Status.** Sanctioned world, added to DESIGN.md's scoped-world list on 2026-09-08 by\nJoshua's decision. The audit found the classroom running on its own typeface and a warm\npalette while the app-wide record described the Workroom as neutral and Lexend-only. The\ndecision was that the code was right and the record was stale. This file is the record\ncatching up.\n\n## What makes it its own world\n\n**A warm paper ground, not the Workroom's neutral.** `--classroom-page` is `#ffffff`\nwith `--classroom-line` at `#f3e3d6` and `--classroom-hover` a warm translucent cream.\nThe rail reads as a study rather than an admin panel, and both independent design\nassessments in the 2026-09-08 audit named that warmth as a genuine asset rather than\ndrift.\n\n**Outfit, not Lexend.** `classroom.css` sets `font-family: \"Outfit\"` on the wrapper. The\napp-wide Workroom face is Lexend, and Lexend renders nowhere inside the classroom. That\nis deliberate as of this brief. Outfit is also the question lock, so LSAT text and the\nroom it sits in share a face, and a passage does not change shape when the student moves\nbetween reading a note and reading a stimulus.\n\n**IBM Plex Mono for instrument labels.** The rail group labels, the chevrons, the brand\nlockup and the unread badge. Swapped from Roboto Mono on 2026-09-08: Roboto Mono was\nundeclared anywhere in the design record, and IBM Plex Mono is the mono this project\nalready documents and already loads.\n\n## Tokens this world owns\n\n| Token | Light | Dark | Use |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `--classroom-page` | `#ffffff` | `#0f0e17` | the ground |\n| `--classroom-surface` | `#ffffff` | `#0a0913` | panel and card ground |\n| `--classroom-ink` | `#43403c` | `#fffffe` | primary text |\n| `--classroom-ink-body` | `#43403c` | `#e5e5e5` | body copy |\n| `--classroom-meta-text` | `#6d6a67` | `#a7a9be` | **any text that is muted** |\n| `--classroom-meta-soft` | `rgba(67,64,60,.4)` | `#a7a9be` | **fills and borders only, never text** |\n| `--classroom-line` | `#f3e3d6` | `#17161c` | hairline separation |\n| `--classroom-line-strong` | `#9c8f85` | `#646176` | a border that is itself the affordance (WCAG 1.4.11) |\n| `--classroom-accent` | `#a25704` | `#ff8906` | a fill that carries text: primary button, unread badge, unread dot |\n| `--classroom-accent-ink` | `#ffffff` | `#0f0e17` | the label on that fill |\n| `--classroom-link` | `#ff8906` | `#ff8906` | **decorative fills only** |\n| `--classroom-link-text` | `#a25704` | `#ff8906` | orange that carries words |\n\n## Named rules\n\n**The text/fill split is the load-bearing rule here.** `--classroom-meta` and\n`--classroom-meta-soft` are tuned as fills and measure 1.85:1 and 2.10:1 as text on the\npage. Every muted *word* resolves from `--classroom-meta-text`. The same split governs\n`--classroom-link` and `--classroom-link-text`. Getting this wrong put every date, every\nrail group label and the unread badge below the AA floor for months.\n\n**Opacity multiplies against the colour.** Swapping a token is not enough if an\n`opacity` sits on the same rule. `.classroom-nav-group > b` was `#6d6a67` at `0.7` and\nstill measured 2.92:1. Muted text carries its colour in the token, not in an alpha.\n\n**A themed pair, not a shared colour, for anything on an accent fill.** The amber that\nworks on the light ground is `#a25704`; on the dark ground it is `#ff8906` with near-black\non top. Reusing one value across both themes trades a light failure for a dark one.\n\n**Never show a control that opens a copy of what is on screen.** Below 1280px the stage\nrenders its own `StageIndexList`; the \"Open the list\" trigger is suppressed by\n`.classroom-column:has(.classroom-stage-index)`. The trigger belongs to detail routes,\nwhere nothing else reaches the list.\n\n**Overlays are dialogs.** The section drawer and the mobile menu go through\n`useDialogOverlay`: `role=\"dialog\"`, `aria-modal`, an accessible name, focus moved in on\nopen, a focus trap that wraps both ways, Escape, and focus restored to the trigger. Focus\nis moved on a timer rather than `requestAnimationFrame`, because rAF does not fire while\nthe document is not being rendered.\n\n**Index routes carry no dateline.** It printed `new Date()`, so it stamped the page with\nthe day of the visit while occupying the slot that, on a note, holds the day Joshua wrote\nit. Every number on a surface traces to stored data or it does not appear.\n\n## Anti-references\n\nNot a dashboard. Not an LMS. No progress rings, no streak badges, no card grid of\nmodules, no metric tiles. The classroom is a room with a desk in it, and the thing on the\ndesk is what the coach wrote.\n"}

{"replace_all": false, "file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\components\\classroom\\ClassroomRouteViews.tsx", "old_string": "function DetailLoading() {", "new_string": "/** Below this, a flat list is easier to scan than a grouped one. */\nconst MONTH_GROUP_THRESHOLD = 12;\n\n/**\n * Read notes bucketed by the month they were written, newest month first.\n * Input is already sorted newest-first by groupNotes, so insertion order is\n * the display order and no second sort is needed.\n */\nfunction groupNotesByMonth<T extends { created_at: string }>(notes: T[]) {\n  const buckets = new Map<string, { key: string; label: string; notes: T[] }>();\n  for (const note of notes) {\n    const date = new Date(note.created_at);\n    const key = `${date.getFullYear()}-${date.getMonth()}`;\n    let bucket = buckets.get(key);\n    if (!bucket) {\n      bucket = {\n        key,\n        label: new Intl.DateTimeFormat(\"en-CA\", {\n          month: \"long\",\n          year: \"numeric\",\n        }).format(date),\n        notes: [],\n      };\n      buckets.set(key, bucket);\n    }\n    bucket.notes.push(note);\n  }\n  return [...buckets.values()];\n}\n\nfunction DetailLoading() {"}
